It feels like I just… don’t need it.\n\nThere have been a lot of weeks where I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, sometimes less if I’m really locked in on work stuff or thinking through what I need to do next, and then I wake up like I’m already plugged into something. Like my eyes open and I’m ready, wired, thinking, okay, I can use this time, I can get ahead, I can figure things out before everyone else is even awake.\n\nAnd lying in bed feels almost annoying, like it’s a waste. I’ll be sitting there thinking, why am I just staring at the ceiling when I could be making moves, checking numbers, planning orders, doing something useful. It’s not like a normal bad night where you feel wrecked the next day. In those stretches I feel rested, or at least I feel like I don’t need rest.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people are tracking or sabotaging you ever happened during a time when your mood was normal?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that has happened, which is part of why it scares me, honestly.\n\nBecause I could understand it more if it only happened when I was barely sleeping and feeling like I was ahead of everybody, like I had the whole market figured out or whatever. But there was this calmer stretch where I wasn’t really feeling like that. I was sleeping more normal hours, not waking up all wired, not feeling like I was running circles around everyone.\n\nAnd even then, for a few weeks, I still had this thing where I was convinced people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered, trying to figure out my moves, maybe messing with me or setting me up to look bad. And the voice was still happening in the stockroom sometimes. Not like constantly, but enough that I remember thinking, okay, I’m not even in that super keyed-up place right now, so why is this still going on?\n\nSo… yeah. It’s not only when my mood is up. That’s the part that makes it feel harder to explain away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it exactly like that. I’ve had stretches where I felt really down, like heavy and not myself, but the “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down.\n\nTime kind of blurs when I’m in it, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I know there have been days where I’m just dragging and everything feels pointless or flat, but whether it was a full two weeks straight… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really track it that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice or the belief that people were tracking or sabotaging you ever lasted for at least two weeks during a time when you were not feeling especially up or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s what I was trying to say before, but I think I didn’t say it clearly enough.\n\nThere was a stretch where I wasn’t especially up, like I wasn’t doing that thing where I’m barely sleeping and feeling like I can see the whole market better than everyone. I was sleeping more normally, going through work more normally, not feeling like I was running on that charged-up energy.\n\nBut even then, it kept going for a few weeks. I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered or trying to figure out what I was doing, maybe sabotaging me somehow. And I was still hearing the voice in the stockroom sometimes. It wasn’t every second of the day, but it was enough that it didn’t feel like just one weird moment.\n\nSo yeah, it lasted at least a couple weeks during a calmer period, when I wasn’t especially up or really depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to answer that cleanly.\n\nLike, I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low and kind of weighed down, and it felt like it was lasting longer than just a bad day or two. But when you say “at least two weeks” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself, because I don’t really keep track that precisely. When I’m in that kind of state, the days sort of run together, and afterward I’m not always good at saying, okay, this started on Monday and ended two Fridays later.\n\nSo… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes like I’m certain. I know I’ve had stretches of feeling depressed or flat, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you ever lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I know when I’m low, I don’t feel as excited about things, but I can’t tell if it’s like “almost everything” or if it’s more just that everything feels harder to start.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll still do something I normally like, but it feels kind of muted, like I’m going through the motions. Other times I just don’t bother because it seems like too much effort. But I don’t know if that counts as losing pleasure, or if it’s more just being tired and flat.\n\nSo I guess I’ve noticed some drop-off, but I can’t confidently say I lost interest or pleasure in almost everything. I don’t have a clear enough memory of it to say yes for sure.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you feel sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that exactly. “Sad or empty most of the day nearly every day” sounds so specific, and I don’t want to just agree because it sounds close.\n\nI remember feeling kind of heavy, like dulled out or low, and there were days where I didn’t really feel like myself. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what really made me come in is this thing at work that I can’t get out of my head. I’m a buyer, so I’m dealing with product launches and numbers and vendors and all that, and lately I keep feeling like people at work — and even some of the vendors — are tracking what I’m buying, like watching my buys way too closely.\n\nAnd it’s not just normal checking or reports or whatever. It feels like they’re messing with the launch numbers on purpose, like shifting things around so it looks like I made bad calls or like I don’t know what I’m doing. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, extreme, but it feels really real when I’m looking at the numbers and then suddenly something doesn’t match what I thought it should be.\n\nMy supervisor has shown me reports and tried to explain that there isn’t proof anyone’s doing anything, but I still can’t shake it. Even when I’m sitting there nodding like, okay, I see what you’re saying, part of me is still thinking, yeah, but what if the report is just part of it too, or what if they’re covering it up better than I can see. It’s been stressing me out a lot, and I guess I’m here because I don’t know how to make that feeling stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, energetic, or like you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it “high” exactly, but there are definitely stretches where I feel like everything is opening up, like the whole city is kind of… available to me, if that makes sense. Like I’m moving faster than everybody else and I can see opportunities other people are missing.\n\nAnd at work, that can feel good at first, because I’m like, okay, I know what to buy, I know what’s going to hit, I can make these decisions. But then if someone questions a purchase order or tells me to slow down, it’s like I snap way harder than I mean to. Like they’re not just asking a normal question, it feels like they’re trying to block me or make me look stupid, and I get really irritated really fast.\n\nSo yeah, there are times I feel really energized and keyed up, but it can turn into me being angry pretty quick if someone gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s happened. Not all the time, but enough that it freaks me out.\n\nLike, sometimes if I’m in the stockroom by myself, or I’m driving back after meeting with a vendor, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. It’s not like a thought exactly — it feels more outside of me, like someone is there. He’ll say stuff like, “Move faster, Scott,” or he’ll kind of laugh, like he’s laughing at what I picked or what I ordered.\n\nAnd I’ll actually turn around, or check the mirrors if I’m in the car, because it feels that close. But nobody’s there. Then I feel stupid for looking, but in the moment it really sounds like it’s coming from behind me.\n\nDoctor: During these energized periods, do you sleep much less than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s actually one of the things that makes it hard for me to tell if something’s wrong, because during those times it doesn’t feel like I’m tired and pushing through it. It feels like I just… don’t need it.\n\nThere have been a lot of weeks where I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, sometimes less if I’m really locked in on work stuff or thinking through what I need to do next, and then I wake up like I’m already plugged into something. Like my eyes open and I’m ready, wired, thinking, okay, I can use this time, I can get ahead, I can figure things out before everyone else is even awake.\n\nAnd lying in bed feels almost annoying, like it’s a waste. I’ll be sitting there thinking, why am I just staring at the ceiling when I could be making moves, checking numbers, planning orders, doing something useful. It’s not like a normal bad night where you feel wrecked the next day. In those stretches I feel rested, or at least I feel like I don’t need rest.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people are tracking or sabotaging you ever happened during a time when your mood was normal?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that has happened, which is part of why it scares me, honestly.\n\nBecause I could understand it more if it only happened when I was barely sleeping and feeling like I was ahead of everybody, like I had the whole market figured out or whatever. But there was this calmer stretch where I wasn’t really feeling like that. I was sleeping more normal hours, not waking up all wired, not feeling like I was running circles around everyone.\n\nAnd even then, for a few weeks, I still had this thing where I was convinced people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered, trying to figure out my moves, maybe messing with me or setting me up to look bad. And the voice was still happening in the stockroom sometimes. Not like constantly, but enough that I remember thinking, okay, I’m not even in that super keyed-up place right now, so why is this still going on?\n\nSo… yeah. It’s not only when my mood is up. That’s the part that makes it feel harder to explain away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it exactly like that. I’ve had stretches where I felt really down, like heavy and not myself, but the “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down.\n\nTime kind of blurs when I’m in it, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I know there have been days where I’m just dragging and everything feels pointless or flat, but whether it was a full two weeks straight… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really track it that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice or the belief that people were tracking or sabotaging you ever lasted for at least two weeks during a time when you were not feeling especially up or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s what I was trying to say before, but I think I didn’t say it clearly enough.\n\nThere was a stretch where I wasn’t especially up, like I wasn’t doing that thing where I’m barely sleeping and feeling like I can see the whole market better than everyone. I was sleeping more normally, going through work more normally, not feeling like I was running on that charged-up energy.\n\nBut even then, it kept going for a few weeks. I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered or trying to figure out what I was doing, maybe sabotaging me somehow. And I was still hearing the voice in the stockroom sometimes. It wasn’t every second of the day, but it was enough that it didn’t feel like just one weird moment.\n\nSo yeah, it lasted at least a couple weeks during a calmer period, when I wasn’t especially up or really depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to answer that cleanly.\n\nLike, I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low and kind of weighed down, and it felt like it was lasting longer than just a bad day or two. But when you say “at least two weeks” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself, because I don’t really keep track that precisely. When I’m in that kind of state, the days sort of run together, and afterward I’m not always good at saying, okay, this started on Monday and ended two Fridays later.\n\nSo… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes like I’m certain. I know I’ve had stretches of feeling depressed or flat, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you ever lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I know when I’m low, I don’t feel as excited about things, but I can’t tell if it’s like “almost everything” or if it’s more just that everything feels harder to start.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll still do something I normally like, but it feels kind of muted, like I’m going through the motions. Other times I just don’t bother because it seems like too much effort. But I don’t know if that counts as losing pleasure, or if it’s more just being tired and flat.\n\nSo I guess I’ve noticed some drop-off, but I can’t confidently say I lost interest or pleasure in almost everything. I don’t have a clear enough memory of it to say yes for sure.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you feel sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that exactly. “Sad or empty most of the day nearly every day” sounds so specific, and I don’t want to just agree because it sounds close.\n\nI remember feeling kind of heavy, like dulled out or low, and there were days where I didn’t really feel like myself. But was it most of the day, almost every day, for a whole stretch? I’m not sure. Sometimes it was more like I’d feel okay for part of the day and then it would drop later, or I’d wake up feeling blank and then get distracted by work or something.\n\nSo… I guess I had low or empty-ish feelings at times, but I can’t honestly say I clearly remember it being most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt depressed nearly every day and also either lost interest in almost everything or felt no pleasure in it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give a clean yes to that. I know that probably sounds like I’m being evasive, but I’m really trying to be accurate.\n\nI’ve had periods where I felt down for a while, and during those times things I usually like didn’t really feel the same. But when you put both parts together — like depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks, and also losing interest in almost everything or feeling no pleasure — I just don’t have a clear enough memory to say yes, that definitely happened.\n\nIt’s more like… I remember stretches where I was low and flat, and I remember not wanting to do much, but I can’t separate whether that was actual loss of pleasure or just me being worn out and not motivated. And I also don’t know if it was nearly every day for two full weeks. I don’t track my moods that specifically, so afterward it gets blurry.\n\nSo I’d say maybe, but not confidently. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: At any point in your life, have you had a period of 2 weeks or longer when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. Like, I’ve definitely had times where I felt down for a while — not just one bad day — but when you say “2 weeks or longer” and “depressed nearly every day,” that’s where I get stuck.\n\nI don’t really keep track of it that way, like marking days on a calendar. Looking back, it all kind of blends together. There were stretches where I’d wake up feeling low or heavy, and I’d think, “Okay, this is still happening,” but I don’t know if that was every day or nearly every day for two full weeks. Some days might have been more normal, or I might have just been distracted enough that I didn’t notice it as much.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… maybe, but I can’t confidently say yes. I’ve had longer low periods, but I’m not sure they clearly meet that “nearly every day for at least two weeks” thing.\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, did you feel more important or powerful than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those energized times, I do get that way. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud because when I’m calmer I can see how overblown it sounds, but in the moment it feels completely obvious to me.\n\nLike with work, I’ll start thinking I can spot the next trend before anyone else in L.A. can. Not just like, “Oh, I have a good eye,” but more like I’m convinced I’m seeing the whole sneaker market clearly and everybody else is behind. I’ll think the market is basically waiting on my calls, like if I decide a certain shoe or style is going to hit, then that’s what’s going to happen.\n\nAnd the weird part is I’m still one of the junior buyers, so it’s not like I’m actually in charge of everything. I know that. But when I’m in that state, it feels like my opinions matter way more than they realistically do, like I’m some kind of key person driving what’s next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that life was not worth living?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that the right way. I mean… if you mean like a clear thought of “life is not worth living,” I don’t know that I’ve really tracked my thoughts in those exact words.\n\nThere have been times where I felt really worn down or kind of stuck, and I might think things like, “What is the point of all this?” but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually believing life isn’t worth living. Sometimes it’s more like frustration or exhaustion in the moment than a specific thought.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if what you mean is something more definite than what I’ve noticed in myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that before, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I think the honest answer is still that I’m not completely sure.\n\nI’ve definitely had periods where I felt low for a while, like more than just having a bad day or two. But when you say “nearly every day for two weeks or longer,” I get stuck because I don’t really remember it in that clean of a way. I don’t keep track like, “Okay, day seven, still depressed,” or anything like that.\n\nThere were stretches where I’d wake up and feel kind of heavy or unmotivated, and it felt like it was hanging around, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks. Some days might have been okay, or I might have been busy enough that I didn’t notice it as much. So… maybe, but I don’t feel sure enough to say yes clearly.\n\nDoctor: When the voice was happening during that calmer stretch, were you feeling depressed at that time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. At least I don’t remember feeling depressed then. That’s part of what made it confusing, because it wasn’t during one of those times where I was super up and convinced I was basically running the whole market, but it also wasn’t like I was in some deep low.\n\nI was actually sleeping more normally during that stretch, and I felt calmer overall. Like I wasn’t buzzing with ideas or feeling like every decision I made was going to move the sneaker world. But even with that, for a few weeks I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys, like watching what I ordered or noticing patterns in what I was choosing. And I was still hearing the voice in the stockroom.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I was depressed at the same time the voice was happening during that calmer period, I’d say no, not really. I was calmer, but the tracking thing and the voice were still there.\n\nDoctor: When you were feeling depressed, did you ever hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’m trying to separate it out because sometimes the voice happened when I wasn’t really depressed, like I said, but there were other times when I was feeling low or worn down and I still heard it.\n\nIt’s usually not like a whole conversation, if that makes sense. It’s more like I’ll be in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, and I hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. Like close enough that I’ll actually turn my head or check the mirror. It’ll say something like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at what I picked, like it’s mocking my choices. And then there’s nobody there.\n\nWhen I’m already feeling depressed, it hits harder, I guess. Like instead of just being startled or annoyed, I start feeling more embarrassed or stupid about it, because it feels like I can’t even do my job without being watched or criticized. During the low periods, I remember feeling depressed for big chunks of time, like waking up with that heavy feeling and carrying it around through the day, but “nearly all day, almost every day” is hard for me to say for sure.\n\nSome days it felt like it was just there in the background all day, even if I was still doing things. I don’t remember it that precisely.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’ll think the market is basically waiting on my calls, like if I decide a certain shoe or style is going to hit, then that’s what’s going to happen.\n\nAnd the weird part is I’m still one of the junior buyers, so it’s not like I’m actually in charge of everything. I know that. But when I’m in that state, it feels like my opinions matter way more than they realistically do, like I’m some kind of key person driving what’s next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that life was not worth living?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that the right way. I mean… if you mean like a clear thought of “life is not worth living,” I don’t know that I’ve really tracked my thoughts in those exact words.\n\nThere have been times where I felt really worn down or kind of stuck, and I might think things like, “What is the point of all this?” but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually believing life isn’t worth living. Sometimes it’s more like frustration or exhaustion in the moment than a specific thought.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if what you mean is something more definite than what I’ve noticed in myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that before, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I think the honest answer is still that I’m not completely sure.\n\nI’ve definitely had periods where I felt low for a while, like more than just having a bad day or two. But when you say “nearly every day for two weeks or longer,” I get stuck because I don’t really remember it in that clean of a way. I don’t keep track like, “Okay, day seven, still depressed,” or anything like that.\n\nThere were stretches where I’d wake up and feel kind of heavy or unmotivated, and it felt like it was hanging around, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks. Some days might have been okay, or I might have been busy enough that I didn’t notice it as much. So… maybe, but I don’t feel sure enough to say yes clearly.\n\nDoctor: When the voice was happening during that calmer stretch, were you feeling depressed at that time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. At least I don’t remember feeling depressed then. That’s part of what made it confusing, because it wasn’t during one of those times where I was super up and convinced I was basically running the whole market, but it also wasn’t like I was in some deep low.\n\nI was actually sleeping more normally during that stretch, and I felt calmer overall. Like I wasn’t buzzing with ideas or feeling like every decision I made was going to move the sneaker world. But even with that, for a few weeks I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys, like watching what I ordered or noticing patterns in what I was choosing. And I was still hearing the voice in the stockroom.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I was depressed at the same time the voice was happening during that calmer period, I’d say no, not really. I was calmer, but the tracking thing and the voice were still there.\n\nDoctor: When you were feeling depressed, did you ever hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’m trying to separate it out because sometimes the voice happened when I wasn’t really depressed, like I said, but there were other times when I was feeling low or worn down and I still heard it.\n\nIt’s usually not like a whole conversation, if that makes sense. It’s more like I’ll be in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, and I hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. Like close enough that I’ll actually turn my head or check the mirror. It’ll say something like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at what I picked, like it’s mocking my choices. And then there’s nobody there.\n\nWhen I’m already feeling depressed, it hits harder, I guess. I’m not sure I can say yes to that in a clean way. Like, I’ve had stretches where I felt down a lot, and it would feel like it was following me around for days, maybe longer, but when you say “at least two weeks” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing it.

Not all the time, but enough that it freaks me out.\n\nLike, sometimes if I’m in the stockroom by myself, or I’m driving back after meeting with a vendor, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. He’ll say stuff like, “Move faster, Scott,” or he’ll kind of laugh, like he’s laughing at what I picked or what I ordered.\n\nAnd I’ll actually turn around, or check the mirrors if I’m in the car, because it feels that close. But nobody’s there. Then I feel stupid for looking, but in the moment it really sounds like it’s coming from behind me.\n\nDoctor: During these energized periods, do you sleep much less than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It feels like I just… don’t need it.\n\nThere have been a lot of weeks where I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, sometimes less if I’m really locked in on work stuff or thinking through what I need to do next, and then I wake up like I’m already plugged into something. Like my eyes open and I’m ready, wired, thinking, okay, I can use this time, I can get ahead, I can figure things out before everyone else is even awake.\n\nAnd lying in bed feels almost annoying, like it’s a waste. But there was this calmer stretch where I wasn’t really feeling like that. I know there have been days where I’m just dragging and everything feels pointless or flat, but whether it was a full two weeks straight… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really track it that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice or the belief that people were tracking or sabotaging you ever lasted for at least two weeks during a time when you were not feeling especially up or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered or trying to figure out what I was doing, maybe sabotaging me somehow. But when you say “at least two weeks” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself, because I don’t really keep track that precisely. When I’m in that kind of state, the days sort of run together, and afterward I’m not always good at saying, okay, this started on Monday and ended two Fridays later.\n\nSo… maybe? But I don’t want to say yes like I’m certain. I know I’ve had stretches of feeling depressed or flat, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you ever lose interest or pleasure in almost everything you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I know when I’m low, I don’t feel as excited about things, but I can’t tell if it’s like “almost everything” or if it’s more just that everything feels harder to start.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll still do something I normally like, but it feels kind of muted, like I’m going through the motions. Other times I just don’t bother because it seems like too much effort. But I don’t know if that counts as losing pleasure, or if it’s more just being tired and flat.\n\nSo I guess I’ve noticed some drop-off, but I can’t confidently say I lost interest or pleasure in almost everything. I don’t have a clear enough memory of it to say yes for sure.\n\nDoctor: During one of those low periods, did you feel sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that exactly. “Sad or empty most of the day nearly every day” sounds so specific, and I don’t want to just agree because it sounds close.\n\nI remember feeling kind of heavy, like dulled out or low, and there were days where I didn’t really feel like myself. But was it most of the day, almost every day, for a whole stretch? I’m not sure. Sometimes it was more like I’d feel okay for part of the day and then it would drop later, or I’d wake up feeling blank and then get distracted by work or something.\n\nSo… I guess I had low or empty-ish feelings at times, but I can’t honestly say I clearly remember it being most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt depressed nearly every day and also either lost interest in almost everything or felt no pleasure in it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give a clean yes to that. I know that probably sounds like I’m being evasive, but I’m really trying to be accurate.\n\nI’ve had periods where I felt down for a while, and during those times things I usually like didn’t really feel the same. But when you put both parts together — like depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks, and also losing interest in almost everything or feeling no pleasure — I just don’t have a clear enough memory to say yes, that definitely happened.\n\nIt’s more like… I remember stretches where I was low and flat, and I remember not wanting to do much, but I can’t separate whether that was actual loss of pleasure or just me being worn out and not motivated. And I also don’t know if it was nearly every day for two full weeks. I don’t track my moods that specifically, so afterward it gets blurry.\n\nSo I’d say maybe, but not confidently. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: At any point in your life, have you had a period of 2 weeks or longer when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. Like, I’ve definitely had times where I felt down for a while — not just one bad day — but when you say “2 weeks or longer” and “depressed nearly every day,” that’s where I get stuck.\n\nI don’t really keep track of it that way, like marking days on a calendar. Looking back, it all kind of blends together. There were stretches where I’d wake up feeling low or heavy, and I’d think, “Okay, this is still happening,” but I don’t know if that was every day or nearly every day for two full weeks. Some days might have been more normal, or I might have just been distracted enough that I didn’t notice it as much.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… maybe, but I can’t confidently say yes. I’ve had longer low periods, but I’m not sure they clearly meet that “nearly every day for at least two weeks” thing.\n\nDoctor: During those energized times, did you feel more important or powerful than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those energized times, I do get that way. It’s kind of embarrassing to say out loud because when I’m calmer I can see how overblown it sounds, but in the moment it feels completely obvious to me.\n\nLike with work, I’ll start thinking I can spot the next trend before anyone else in L.A. can. Not just like, “Oh, I have a good eye,” but more like I’m convinced I’m seeing the whole sneaker market clearly and everybody else is behind. I’ll think the market is basically waiting on my calls, like if I decide a certain shoe or style is going to hit, then that’s what’s going to happen.\n\nAnd the weird part is I’m still one of the junior buyers, so it’s not like I’m actually in charge of everything. I know that. But when I’m in that state, it feels like my opinions matter way more than they realistically do, like I’m some kind of key person driving what’s next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that life was not worth living?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that the right way. I mean… if you mean like a clear thought of “life is not worth living,” I don’t know that I’ve really tracked my thoughts in those exact words.\n\nThere have been times where I felt really worn down or kind of stuck, and I might think things like, “What is the point of all this?” but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually believing life isn’t worth living. Sometimes it’s more like frustration or exhaustion in the moment than a specific thought.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if what you mean is something more definite than what I’ve noticed in myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that before, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I think the honest answer is still that I’m not completely sure.\n\nI’ve definitely had periods where I felt low for a while, like more than just having a bad day or two. But when you say “nearly every day for two weeks or longer,” I get stuck because I don’t really remember it in that clean of a way. I don’t keep track like, “Okay, day seven, still depressed,” or anything like that.\n\nThere were stretches where I’d wake up and feel kind of heavy or unmotivated, and it felt like it was hanging around, but I can’t confidently say it was nearly every day for a full two weeks. Some days might have been okay, or I might have been busy enough that I didn’t notice it as much. So… maybe, but I don’t feel sure enough to say yes clearly.\n\nDoctor: When the voice was happening during that calmer stretch, were you feeling depressed at that time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. At least I don’t remember feeling depressed then. That’s part of what made it confusing, because it wasn’t during one of those times where I was super up and convinced I was basically running the whole market, but it also wasn’t like I was in some deep low.\n\nI was actually sleeping more normally during that stretch, and I felt calmer overall. Like I wasn’t buzzing with ideas or feeling like every decision I made was going to move the sneaker world. But even with that, for a few weeks I still had this belief that people were tracking my buys, like watching what I ordered or noticing patterns in what I was choosing. And I was still hearing the voice in the stockroom.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I was depressed at the same time the voice was happening during that calmer period, I’d say no, not really. I was calmer, but the tracking thing and the voice were still there.\n\nDoctor: When you were feeling depressed, did you ever hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’m trying to separate it out because sometimes the voice happened when I wasn’t really depressed, like I said, but there were other times when I was feeling low or worn down and I still heard it.\n\nIt’s usually not like a whole conversation, if that makes sense. It’s more like I’ll be in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, and I hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. Like close enough that I’ll actually turn my head or check the mirror. It’ll say something like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at what I picked, like it’s mocking my choices. And then there’s nobody there.\n\nWhen I’m already feeling depressed, it hits harder, I guess. Like instead of just being startled or annoyed, I start feeling more embarrassed or stupid about it, because it feels like I can’t even do my job without being watched or criticized. During the low periods, I remember feeling depressed for big chunks of time, like waking up with that heavy feeling and carrying it around through the day, but “nearly all day, almost every day” is hard for me to say for sure.\n\nSome days it felt like it was just there in the background all day, even if I was still doing things. Yeah… I think for most of it, yes. Not every single day in the exact same way, but if I’m looking at the whole stretch, the suspicious stuff and the voice have kind of been around through most of it.

But there was this calmer stretch where I wasn’t really feeling like that. I was sleeping more normal hours, not waking up all wired, not feeling like I was running circles around everyone.\n\nAnd even then, for a few weeks, I still had this thing where I was convinced people were tracking my buys — like watching what I ordered, trying to figure out my moves, maybe messing with me or setting me up to look bad. I’ve had stretches where I felt really down, like heavy and not myself, but the “most of the day nearly every day for two weeks” part is hard for me to pin down.\n\nTime kind of blurs when I’m in it, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. But was it most of the day, almost every day, for a whole stretch? I’m not sure. Sometimes it was more like I’d feel okay for part of the day and then it would drop later, or I’d wake up feeling blank and then get distracted by work or something.\n\nSo… I guess I had low or empty-ish feelings at times, but I can’t honestly say I clearly remember it being most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for at least two weeks, you felt depressed nearly every day and also either lost interest in almost everything or felt no pleasure in it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give a clean yes to that. I know that probably sounds like I’m being evasive, but I’m really trying to be accurate.\n\nI’ve had periods where I felt down for a while, and during those times things I usually like didn’t really feel the same.
